| 2025‑08‑07 | [Cognitive Duality for Adaptive Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.05081) | web | Introduces CogniWeb, which switches between fast reactive processing and slower deliberative reasoning according to task complexity. WebArena experiments examine this combination of learned behaviors and online planning. |
| 2025‑08‑06 | [Beyond Pixels: Exploring DOM Downsampling for LLM-Based Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.04412) | web | D2Snap reduces serialized DOM observations while preserving actionable elements and features that distinguish their uses. The study compares reduced DOM inputs with screenshot-based observations for a web agent on tasks sampled from Online-Mind2Web. |
| 2025‑08‑06 | [HarmonyGuard: Toward Safety and Utility in Web Agents via Adaptive Policy Enhancement and Dual-Objective Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.04010) | web | Introduces HarmonyGuard, a multi-agent framework that updates security policies and weighs safety against task completion for agents operating on the open web. |
| 2025‑08‑06 | [VeriWeb: Verifiable Long-Chain Web Benchmark for Agentic Information-Seeking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.04026) | web | Introduces VeriWeb, a benchmark of long-chain web information-seeking tasks with verifiable subtasks and a complete human-annotated trajectory for each task. |
| 2025‑08‑03 | [Web-CogReasoner: Towards Multimodal Knowledge-Induced Cognitive Reasoning for Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.01858) | web | Web-CogReasoner separates factual and conceptual web knowledge from procedural reasoning, using a structured curriculum to train a multimodal agent. Web-CogDataset and Web-CogBench support study of these capabilities alongside web-navigation evaluation. |
| 2025‑07‑31 | [General Agentic Planning Through Simulative Reasoning with World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23773) | web | Introduces SiRA, an agent architecture that uses a language-based world model to simulate action outcomes for planning in web-browser tasks. |
| 2025‑07‑30 | [Magentic-UI: Towards Human-in-the-loop Agentic Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.22358) | web | Magentic-UI combines browser interaction, code execution, file operations, and MCP tools in an interface for human-agent collaboration. It studies collaborative planning and task execution, action guards, and persistent memory through benchmark tasks, simulated users, user studies, and safety assessments. |
| 2025‑07‑25 | [MMBench-GUI: Hierarchical Multi-Platform Evaluation Framework for GUI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.19478) | web, desktop, windows, macos, linux, ios, android, cross-platform | MMBench-GUI evaluates interface understanding, element grounding, task automation, and collaboration across six named desktop, mobile, and web platforms. It introduces the Efficiency-Quality Area metric for examining execution efficiency alongside task quality in online GUI automation. |
| 2025‑07‑20 | [Manipulating LLM Web Agents with Indirect Prompt Injection Attack via HTML Accessibility Tree](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.14799) | web | Studies indirect prompt injection through HTML content consumed as accessibility trees. The evaluation tests how manipulated page observations redirect browser-agent actions on real websites. |
| 2025‑07‑18 | [WebGuard: Building a Generalizable Guardrail for Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.14293) | web | Introduces WebGuard, a human-annotated dataset for predicting outcomes and risk levels of state-changing website actions. It provides generalization splits and evaluates specialized guardrail models. |
| 2025‑07‑17 | [Machine-Readable Ads: Accessibility and Trust Patterns for AI Web Agents interacting with Online Advertisements](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.12844) | web | Compares DOM-based and pixel-based web agents on a cloned news site containing advertisements, consent dialogs and paywalls. The study examines how interface presentation affects ad interaction and consequential subscription choices. |
| 2025‑07‑15 | [Let's Think in Two Steps: Mitigating Agreement Bias in MLLMs with Self-Grounded Verification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.11662) | web | Studies agreement bias in multimodal agent verifiers across web navigation and computer use, and introduces SGV to assess trajectories using self-generated priors. |
| 2025‑07‑09 | [VisualTrap: A Stealthy Backdoor Attack on GUI Agents via Visual Grounding Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.06899) | web, desktop | Studies backdoors that redirect GUI visual grounding even when the task plan is correct. VisualTrap evaluates whether poisoned grounding training transfers to downstream tasks and across web, mobile, and desktop interface settings. |
| 2025‑07‑08 | [OpenAgentSafety: A Comprehensive Framework for Evaluating Real-World AI Agent Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.06134) | web, desktop | Evaluates agent safety across real browser, code, filesystem, shell, and messaging tools using rule-based and model-based assessments. |
| 2025‑07‑08 | [R-VLM: Region-Aware Vision Language Model for Precise GUI Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05673) | web, cross-platform | R-VLM localizes GUI elements through zoomed-in region proposals and an intersection-over-union-aware training objective. It evaluates the grounding model on ScreenSpot and AgentStudio and studies its effect on navigation in AITW and Mind2Web. |
| 2025‑07‑07 | [A Systematization of Security Vulnerabilities in Computer Use Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05445) | web | Analyzes security boundaries in computer-use agents through a risk taxonomy and concrete adversarial case studies. It links observed failures to input provenance, interface-action binding, memory, and delegation, and proposes CUA-specific evaluation principles. |
| 2025‑07‑06 | [WebSynthesis: World-Model-Guided MCTS for Efficient WebUI-Trajectory Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.04370) | web | Introduces WebSynthesis, which uses a learned web world model and tree-based planning to synthesize interaction trajectories for training web agents. |
| 2025‑07‑05 | [How to Train Your LLM Web Agent: A Statistical Diagnosis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.04103) | web | Studies compute allocation for web-agent post-training with teacher-generated supervised fine-tuning followed by on-policy reinforcement learning. It samples 1,370 training configurations and uses bootstrap analysis to examine hyperparameter sensitivity on WorkArena and MiniWoB++. |
| 2025‑07‑04 | [Less is More: Empowering GUI Agent with Context-Aware Simplification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.03730) | web, cross-platform | SimpAgent prunes distracting interface elements with visual masks and compresses interaction history using consistency-based guidance. The study evaluates these two forms of context reduction during GUI navigation in web and mobile environments. |
| 2025‑07‑01 | [WebArxiv: A Reproducible Benchmark for Evaluating Multimodal Web Agents on arXiv Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.00938) | web | WebArxiv provides 510 static-snapshot tasks with deterministic answers for scholarly browsing, including constrained paper retrieval, content extraction, and cross-paper comparison. The study also tests dynamic retrieval of interaction history to address repetitive or incomplete agent reasoning. |
| 2025‑06‑30 | [LineRetriever: Planning-Aware Observation Reduction for Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.00210) | web | LineRetriever uses a language model to select observation lines that matter for upcoming web-navigation actions. It reduces DOM or accessibility-tree context by considering the planning horizon instead of relying solely on semantic similarity. |
| 2025‑06‑30 | [ZonUI-3B: A Lightweight Vision-Language Model for Cross-Resolution GUI Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.23491) | web, desktop, cross-platform | ZonUI-3B trains GUI grounding with a curated 24,000-example collection of mobile, desktop, and web screenshots. Its two-stage procedure first learns across platforms and then specializes on high-resolution images, with ablations on data diversity and redundant examples. |
| 2025‑06‑26 | [Agent-RewardBench: Towards a Unified Benchmark for Reward Modeling across Perception, Planning, and Safety in Real-World Multimodal Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21252) | web | Introduces Agent-RewardBench to evaluate step-level reward models for multimodal agents across perception, planning, and safety; web navigation is among its scenarios. |
| 2025‑06‑26 | [Mind2Web 2: Evaluating Agentic Search with Agent-as-a-Judge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21506) | web | Introduces Mind2Web 2, 130 long-horizon tasks requiring live web browsing and information synthesis, plus an Agent-as-a-Judge framework for grading answers and sources. |
| 2025‑06‑18 | [Context manipulation attacks : Web agents are susceptible to corrupted memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.17318) | web | Studies context manipulation in web agents by altering stored task representations rather than webpage prompts alone. Evaluation on Browser-use and Agent-E examines memory-directed attacks and the implications for secure context handling. |
| 2025‑06‑18 | [Embodied Web Agents: Bridging Physical-Digital Realms for Integrated Agent Intelligence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15677) | web | Introduces environments that couple 3D physical-world simulations with functional web interfaces. The accompanying benchmark evaluates tasks requiring agents to coordinate online information and physical actions across cooking, navigation and shopping scenarios. |
| 2025‑06‑17 | [AgentSynth: Scalable Task Generation for Generalist Computer-Use Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.14205) | desktop, web | AgentSynth composes easily generated subtasks into longer computer-use tasks and trajectories with adjustable difficulty. Its evaluation uses the number of subtasks to probe how agent success changes as task complexity grows. |
| 2025‑06‑12 | [Build the web for agents, not agents for the web](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10953) | web | Proposes Agentic Web Interface (AWI), a website interface intended for agent navigation, and sets out six design principles for web-agent use. |
| 2025‑06‑12 | [Mirage-1: Augmenting and Updating GUI Agent with Hierarchical Multimodal Skills](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10387) | android, web, cross-platform | Mirage-1 organizes interaction trajectories into execution, core, and meta-skills, then uses them to guide Monte Carlo tree search during online GUI tasks. It introduces AndroidLH for long-horizon evaluation and tests the approach on Android and web-navigation benchmarks. |
| 2025‑06‑09 | [EconWebArena: Benchmarking Autonomous Agents on Economic Tasks in Realistic Web Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.08136) | web | Introduces EconWebArena, a curated benchmark of economic-data tasks requiring live website navigation, multimodal interpretation, and precise extraction. It evaluates visual grounding, planning, and interaction design within multistep workflows. |
| 2025‑06‑09 | [Thinking vs. Doing: Agents that Reason by Scaling Test-Time Interaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07976) | web | Test-Time Interaction scales the interaction horizon so web agents can explore, backtrack and revise plans within a rollout. Its online reinforcement-learning curriculum adjusts rollout lengths and is evaluated on WebVoyager and WebArena. |
| 2025‑06‑04 | [Go-Browse: Training Web Agents with Structured Exploration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.03533) | web | Go-Browse frames web-environment exploration as graph search so that information can be reused across data-collection episodes. It collects 10,000 successful trajectories and 40,000 interaction steps on WebArena and uses them to fine-tune a web agent. |
| 2025‑06‑03 | [DeepShop: A Benchmark for Deep Research Shopping Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.02839) | web | DeepShop evaluates shopping agents with evolving query complexity across product attributes, filters and sorting preferences. Its evaluation combines checks of these individual requirements with overall task success. |
| 2025‑06‑03 | [Surfer-H Meets Holo1: Cost-Efficient Web Agent Powered by Open Weights](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.02865) | web | Describes Surfer-H, a web agent paired with the Holo1 vision-language model family trained on web content, synthetic examples, and agent-generated data. The work introduces WebClick for web-interface localization and evaluates browser task execution on WebVoyager. |
| 2025‑06‑03 | [VPI-Bench: Visual Prompt Injection Attacks for Computer-Use Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.02456) | web | Introduces VPI-Bench, interactive web-platform variants containing visually embedded malicious instructions. It evaluates computer-use and browser-use agents under these threats and examines system-prompt defenses. |
| 2025‑06‑02 | [FormFactory: An Interactive Benchmarking Suite for Multimodal Form-Filling Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.01520) | web | Introduces FormFactory, an interactive web-based benchmark for agents filling online forms, with varied field formats and evaluation of instruction-to-field alignment. |
| 2025‑06‑02 | [MLA-Trust: Benchmarking Trustworthiness of Multimodal LLM Agents in GUI Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.01616) | web, android, cross-platform | Benchmarks truthfulness, controllability, safety, and privacy of multimodal LLM agents across interactive web and mobile GUI tasks. |
| 2025‑06‑02 | [WebChoreArena: Evaluating Web Browsing Agents on Realistic Tedious Web Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.01952) | web | Introduces WebChoreArena, 532 web chores built on WebArena environments to evaluate agents’ retention of page information, calculation, and tracking across pages. |
| 2025‑05‑31 | [ResTest: Targeted Coverage of Residual Not-Covered Code Using Large Language Models for Web GUI Testing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.00520) | web | Introduces ResTest, which uses coverage reports and a state transition graph to infer untested web functions, then directs a GUI agent to execute them. |
| 2025‑05‑31 | [RiOSWorld: Benchmarking the Risk of Multimodal Computer-Use Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.00618) | web | Introduces RiOSWorld, a computer-use risk benchmark separating user-originated and environmental threats. It evaluates both risky-goal intent and goal completion across web and other computer applications. |
| 2025‑05‑30 | [Agent-X: Evaluating Deep Multimodal Reasoning in Vision-Centric Agentic Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24876) | web | Introduces Agent-X, a benchmark of multi-step visual reasoning and tool use with step-level grading across six settings, including web browsing. |
| 2025‑05‑30 | [Open CaptchaWorld: A Comprehensive Web-based Platform for Testing and Benchmarking Multimodal LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24878) | web | Open CaptchaWorld evaluates multimodal agents on interactive browser-based CAPTCHA puzzles. Its benchmark distinguishes task success from the depth of visual reasoning and interaction needed to complete a puzzle. |
| 2025‑05‑30 | [VideoCAD: A Dataset and Model for Learning Long-Horizon 3D CAD UI Interactions from Video](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24838) | web | Introduces synthetic CAD interaction videos, a model for learning interface actions from video, and evaluation tasks for spatial and long-horizon reasoning. |
| 2025‑05‑28 | [RedTeamCUA: Realistic Adversarial Testing of Computer-Use Agents in Hybrid Web-OS Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.21936) | web, desktop | RedTeamCUA combines a virtual-machine OS environment with containerized websites for controlled prompt-injection tests spanning both interfaces. Its RTC-Bench contains 864 examples and can initialize agents at injection points to separate attack susceptibility from navigation failures. |
| 2025‑05‑28 | [WorkForceAgent-R1: Incentivizing Reasoning Capability in LLM-based Web Agents via Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.22942) | web | Uses rule-based rewards for output structure and action correctness to train reasoning for enterprise web navigation. |
| 2025‑05‑27 | [AdInject: Real-World Black-Box Attacks on Web Agents via Advertising Delivery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.21499) | web | Studies advertising delivery as an indirect-injection channel for web agents under a black-box threat model. AdInject evaluates how static ad content shaped by website context can redirect browser actions. |
| 2025‑05‑26 | [WebCoT: Enhancing Web Agent Reasoning by Reconstructing Chain-of-Thought in Reflection, Branching, and Rollback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.20013) | web | WebCoT turns reflection, look-ahead, branching and rollback procedures into chain-of-thought training rationales. Fine-tuning on these reconstructed trajectories is evaluated for web-agent self-improvement and transfer across navigation tasks. |
| 2025‑05‑23 | [TransBench: Breaking Barriers for Transferable Graphical User Interface Agents in Dynamic Digital Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17629) | ios, android, web, cross-platform | Introduces TransBench to evaluate GUI grounding transfer across app versions, iOS, Android, Web, and functionally distinct applications. |
| 2025‑05‑22 | [WebAgent-R1: Training Web Agents via End-to-End Multi-Turn Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16421) | web | Trains web agents through asynchronous multi-turn environment interaction, using task-success rewards to optimize complete trajectories. |
| 2025‑05‑21 | [AutoData: A Multi-Agent System for Open Web Data Collection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15859) | web | Introduces AutoData, a multi-agent system that collects web datasets from a natural-language request, and Instruct2DS, a benchmark for live collection across three domains. |
| 2025‑05‑21 | [ReGUIDE: Data Efficient GUI Grounding via Spatial Reasoning and Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15259) | web | Combines self-generated localization reasoning, spatial consistency checks, and inference-time search to improve data-efficient web grounding. |
| 2025‑05‑21 | [Web-Shepherd: Advancing PRMs for Reinforcing Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15277) | web | Web-Shepherd is a process reward model for assessing individual steps of web-navigation trajectories. It introduces a collection of 40,000 step-level preference pairs with checklists and a separate benchmark for comparing reward models, then evaluates verification during WebArena-lite navigation. |
| 2025‑05‑19 | [GEM: Gaussian Embedding Modeling for Out-of-Distribution Detection in GUI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.12842) | desktop, web, cross-platform | GEM detects instructions outside a GUI agent’s capabilities by modeling distances in its input-embedding space with a Gaussian mixture. Experiments across mobile, computer and browser data also examine selective escalation to a cloud model when an input is detected as unfamiliar. |
| 2025‑05‑19 | [The Hidden Dangers of Browsing AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.13076) | web | Outlines an end-to-end threat model and empirical security evaluation across architectural layers of autonomous browser agents. |
| 2025‑05‑16 | [WebInject: Prompt Injection Attack to Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11717) | web | Introduces WebInject, a prompt-injection attack that optimizes rendered webpage pixels to make screenshot-based web agents take attacker-chosen actions. |
| 2025‑05‑15 | [Characterizing Unintended Consequences in Human-GUI Agent Collaboration for Web Browsing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09875) | web | Studies unintended consequences of collaborative web browsing with GUI agents through social-media analysis and interviews. It organizes observed failures, user impacts, and mitigation practices around planning, interface interaction, feedback, privacy, and trust. |
| 2025‑05‑09 | [AgentVigil: Generic Black-Box Red-teaming for Indirect Prompt Injection against LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05849) | web | Introduces AgentVigil, a black-box fuzzing framework for finding indirect prompt injections against LLM agents, including attacks that redirect web navigation. |
| 2025‑05‑06 | [Deep Research Bench: Evaluating AI Web Research Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.06287) | web | Introduces Deep Research Bench, 89 multi-step web research tasks and a frozen collection of scraped pages for evaluating search agents and their traces over time. |
| 2025‑05‑06 | [WebGen-Bench: Evaluating LLMs on Generating Interactive and Functional Websites from Scratch](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.03733) | web | Introduces WebGen-Bench for evaluating agents that generate multi-file websites, using a web-navigation agent to check whether the resulting pages pass interaction tests. |
| 2025‑05‑01 | [Visual Test-time Scaling for GUI Agent Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.00684) | web | RegionFocus adds visual test-time scaling by zooming into selected screenshot regions and maintaining an image-based map of landmarks and actions. It evaluates this grounding procedure with UI-TARS and Qwen2.5-VL on ScreenSpot-Pro and WebVoyager. |
| 2025‑04‑30 | [WebThinker: Empowering Large Reasoning Models with Deep Research Capability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21776) | web | Combines iterative web search and link-following with interleaved reasoning and report drafting for deep research. Its reported web explorer retrieves page content through a crawler rather than controlling a visual browser interface. |
| 2025‑04‑23 | [PixelWeb: The First Web GUI Dataset with Pixel-Wise Labels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.16419) | web | Introduces PixelWeb, a webpage GUI dataset with element images, contours, and pixel masks. Its annotation pipeline combines bitmap channel information with DOM-based visibility and stacking analysis to address occlusion and overlapping elements. |
| 2025‑04‑23 | [WebEvolver: Enhancing Web Agent Self-Improvement with Coevolving World Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21024) | web | WebEvolver trains a co-evolving world model to predict web observations and generate additional agent-training trajectories. The same model supports look-ahead action selection at inference, with evaluation on live web-task benchmarks. |
| 2025‑04‑22 | [WASP: Benchmarking Web Agent Security Against Prompt Injection Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.18575) | web | Introduces WASP, an end-to-end benchmark for prompt-injection security in web agents. Its evaluation separates partial attack progress from completion of the attacker’s full objective. |
| 2025‑04‑18 | [DoomArena: A framework for Testing AI Agents Against Evolving Security Threats](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.14064) | web | Presents a modular security testing framework that plugs into BrowserGym and tool-use benchmarks to evaluate agents under configurable threat models. |
| 2025‑04‑17 | [WebLists: Extracting Structured Information From Complex Interactive Websites Using Executable LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12682) | web | Introduces WebLists, a benchmark for structured extraction from interactive websites, and BardeenAgent, which turns web-agent runs into replayable CSS-selector programs. |
| 2025‑04‑16 | [WebRollback: Enhancing Web Agents with Explicit Rollback Mechanisms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.11788) | web | WebRollback gives a web agent an explicit action for returning to an earlier state in its navigation trajectory. It studies this recovery mechanism on two live web-navigation benchmarks under both zero-shot and fine-tuned settings. |
| 2025‑04‑15 | [REAL: Benchmarking Autonomous Agents on Deterministic Simulations of Real Websites](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.11543) | web | Introduces REAL, a benchmark of deterministic website replicas with 112 tasks covering information retrieval and state-changing browser actions. |
| 2025‑04‑15 | [The Obvious Invisible Threat: LLM-Powered GUI Agents' Vulnerability to Fine-Print Injections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.11281) | web | Characterizes six forms of GUI-content injection and studies their effects on GUI agents and human users using adversarial webpages. The analysis examines privacy failures and the limits of human oversight. |
| 2025‑04‑14 | [Breaking the Data Barrier -- Building GUI Agents Through Task Generalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10127) | web, android | Studies whether mid-training on GUI perception, multimodal reasoning, and text reasoning transfers to GUI-agent planning. Experiments compare 11 task sources and curated mixtures using WebArena and AndroidWorld to investigate training-data choices when GUI trajectories are scarce. |
| 2025‑04‑14 | [RealWebAssist: A Benchmark for Long-Horizon Web Assistance with Real-World Users](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10445) | web | RealWebAssist evaluates web assistance through sequences of instructions collected from real users across multiple websites. It tests intent interpretation, user-specific routines, long-term context, and visual grounding when instructions are ambiguous or evolve during interaction. |
| 2025‑04‑13 | [AgentA/B: Automated and Scalable Web A/BTesting with Interactive LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.09723) | web | Introduces AgentA/B, which uses persona-based LLM agents to navigate real webpages and perform shopping interactions for simulated web A/B tests. |
| 2025‑04‑12 | [Explorer: Robust Collection of Interactable GUI Elements](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.09352) | android, web | Explorer collects interactable GUI elements from a live target application and records user sessions for application-specific model training. It links repeated interface states into a navigation map that supports path planning and voice-directed interaction. |
| 2025‑04‑11 | [AgentRewardBench: Evaluating Automatic Evaluations of Web Agent Trajectories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.08942) | web | AgentRewardBench evaluates automated web-agent judges against expert reviews of 1,302 trajectories from five benchmarks. The annotations cover completion, side effects, and repetition, enabling comparison of LLM judges and rule-based task evaluators. |
| 2025‑04‑09 | [Inducing Programmatic Skills for Agentic Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06821) | web | Introduces agent skill induction (ASI), which creates, checks, and reuses program-based skills for web tasks by combining primitive actions into higher-level routines. |
| 2025‑04‑09 | [SkillWeaver: Web Agents can Self-Improve by Discovering and Honing Skills](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07079) | web | SkillWeaver explores websites, practices interactions, and converts the resulting experience into a reusable library of callable skill APIs. Its WebArena and live-website experiments examine skill refinement, composition, and transfer from stronger agents to weaker ones. |
| 2025‑04‑07 | [On the Robustness of GUI Grounding Models Against Image Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.04716) | web, desktop, cross-platform | Evaluates GUI grounding models under natural image noise, untargeted adversarial perturbations, and targeted image attacks. Experiments cover mobile, desktop, and web interfaces and analyze sensitivity to image resolution and adversarial changes. |
| 2025‑04‑02 | [An Illusion of Progress? Assessing the Current State of Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.01382) | web | Introduces Online-Mind2Web, with 300 tasks across 136 live websites, and examines how benchmark design affects assessments of web agents. It also develops an LLM judge for task completion and compares its judgments with human evaluation. |
| 2025‑04‑02 | [Are Autonomous Web Agents Good Testers?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.01495) | web | Adapts web agents to execute natural-language test steps, verify assertions, and issue test verdicts. The study introduces 113 passing and failing test cases across three offline web applications and compares two agent implementations with qualitative failure analysis. |
| 2025‑03‑26 | [ShieldAgent: Shielding Agents via Verifiable Safety Policy Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.22738) | web | Proposes a guardrail agent that reasons over explicit safety policies and checks action trajectories, with a web-agent safety benchmark. |
| 2025‑03‑26 | [sudo rm -rf agentic_security](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.20279) | desktop, web | Evaluates security failures of computer-use agents under adversarial requests and screen-context manipulation. The study examines refusal behavior while agents perform tasks in desktop and web environments. |
| 2025‑03‑18 | [WebNav: An Intelligent Agent for Voice-Controlled Web Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13843) | web | Introduces WebNav, a web navigation agent that turns natural-language commands into GUI actions using screenshot context, DOM labels, and separate LLMs for planning and action parameters. |
| 2025‑03‑12 | [AgentDAM: Privacy Leakage Evaluation for Autonomous Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09780) | web | Evaluates whether autonomous web agents minimize use of sensitive personal information when completing tasks that expose unnecessary private data. |
| 2025‑03‑12 | [Plan-and-Act: Improving Planning of Agents for Long-Horizon Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09572) | web | Separates planning from execution and creates synthetic plan annotations for training agents on extended web-navigation tasks. |
| 2025‑03‑10 | [BEARCUBS: A benchmark for computer-using web agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.07919) | web | BEARCUBS provides 111 live-web information-seeking questions with short answers and human-validated browsing trajectories. Its tasks require multimodal computer interaction, including video understanding and navigation of interactive 3D content. |
| 2025‑03‑06 | [SafeArena: Evaluating the Safety of Autonomous Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.04957) | web | Pairs benign and harmful web tasks to assess deliberate misuse risks and categorize agent behavior by risk level. |
| 2025‑03‑04 | [ATLaS: Agent Tuning via Learning Critical Steps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02197) | web | Selects critical steps from expert interaction trajectories for targeted agent fine-tuning, studying efficiency and generalization across tasks. |
| 2025‑03‑04 | [Language Models can Self-Improve at State-Value Estimation for Better Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02878) | web | Introduces Self-Taught Lookahead (STL), which trains a value model to predict actions and resulting states for more efficient search, with evaluation on web-agent tasks. |
| 2025‑03‑04 | [LiteWebAgent: The Open-Source Suite for VLM-Based Web-Agent Applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02950) | web | Introduces a modular visual web-agent stack with planning and memory components, exposed through a remote-browser application and Chrome extension. |
| 2025‑03‑04 | [MPO: Boosting LLM Agents with Meta Plan Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02682) | web | Refines reusable high-level meta plans with execution feedback to guide language-agent planning across interactive environments. |
| 2025‑02‑27 | [Why Are Web AI Agents More Vulnerable Than Standalone LLMs? A Security Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.20383) | web | Analyzes web-agent vulnerabilities at the component level, examining goal placement in prompts, multistep action generation, and observations as sources of increased susceptibility. |
| 2025‑02‑25 | [Towards Sustainable Web Agents: A Plea for Transparency and Dedicated Metrics for Energy Consumption](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.17903) | web | Examines energy and carbon-accounting limitations in web-agent evaluation and argues for transparent, dedicated sustainability measurements. |
| 2025‑02‑25 | [WebGames: Challenging General-Purpose Web-Browsing AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.18356) | web | Introduces WebGames, a browser-agent benchmark with 50+ interactive challenges in a self-contained test environment and verifiable solutions. |
| 2025‑02‑24 | [Towards Enterprise-Ready Computer Using Generalist Agent](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.01861) | web | Describes the CUGA enterprise-agent development process, emphasizing iterative evaluation, failure analysis, and refinement on software-task benchmarks. |
| 2025‑02‑20 | [Advancing Language Multi-Agent Learning with Credit Re-Assignment for Interactive Environment Generalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.14496) | web | Introduces CollabUIAgents, a multi-agent RL framework that assigns LLM-based process rewards to help agents generalize across interactive settings including mobile operations and web browsing. |
| 2025‑02‑18 | [UXAgent: An LLM Agent-Based Usability Testing Framework for Web Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.12561) | web | Introduces UXAgent, which uses simulated users and a browser connector to test website usability and present their actions, responses, and recordings to UX researchers. |
| 2025‑02‑17 | [Explorer: Scaling Exploration-driven Web Trajectory Synthesis for Multimodal Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.11357) | web | Synthesizes multimodal web trajectories through exploration and refinement, then trains a browser agent to study the effect of scaling interaction data. |
| 2025‑02‑12 | [WorldGUI: An Interactive Benchmark for Desktop GUI Automation from Any Starting Point](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.08047) | desktop, web | Introduces desktop and web tasks with varied initial states to test planning recovery and adaptability beyond canonical starting configurations. |
| 2025‑02‑11 | [Advancing Autonomous VLM Agents via Variational Subgoal-Conditioned Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.07949) | web, cross-platform | Uses variational subgoal-conditioned reinforcement learning to improve learning efficiency on sparse-reward mobile and web-control tasks. |
| 2025‑02‑11 | [Symbiotic Cooperation for Web Agents: Harnessing Complementary Strengths of Large and Small LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.07942) | web | Couples large- and small-model web-agent exploration with trajectory synthesis and distillation to improve both model types iteratively. |
| 2025‑02‑10 | [InSTA: Towards Internet-Scale Training For Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.06776) | web | InSTA builds a pipeline that creates website tasks, collects web-agent trajectories and filters them by judged success to train smaller web navigation agents. |
| 2025‑02‑06 | [RWKV-UI: UI Understanding with Enhanced Perception and Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.03971) | web | RWKV-UI is a visual language model for high-resolution web interfaces that uses layout detection and reasoning-chain visual prompts to understand pages. |
| 2025‑01‑31 | [Towards Computer-Using Personal Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15515) | web, cross-platform | Proposes computer-using personal agents connected to user-controlled data repositories, discussing privacy, interoperability, and collaborative tasks. |
| 2025‑01‑28 | [CowPilot: A Framework for Autonomous and Human-Agent Collaborative Web Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.16609) | web | Introduces CowPilot, a web-navigation framework where agents suggest actions and users can pause, reject, override, or resume them. It evaluates autonomous and collaborative use through task success and human effort. |
| 2025‑01‑21 | [R2D2: Remembering, Replaying and Dynamic Decision Making with a Reflective Agentic Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.12485) | web | R2D2 gives web agents a replay-buffer map of visited pages and a reflection step for analyzing errors and revising navigation strategies. |
| 2025‑01‑20 | [Agent-R: Training Language Model Agents to Reflect via Iterative Self-Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.11425) | web | Builds self-critique training examples from searched recovery paths to improve agents' ability to detect and correct errors during interaction. |
| 2025‑01‑18 | [Learn-by-interact: A Data-Centric Framework for Self-Adaptive Agents in Realistic Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.10893) | web, desktop, cross-platform | Learn-by-interact synthesizes agent interaction trajectories from documentation and derives instructions from their histories for training or in-context use across web and desktop tasks. |
| 2025‑01‑13 | [WebWalker: Benchmarking LLMs in Web Traversal](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.07572) | web | Introduces WebWalkerQA and an exploration-and-critique framework for gathering information by traversing multiple levels of a website. |
| 2024‑12‑18 | [TheAgentCompany: Benchmarking LLM Agents on Consequential Real World Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.14161) | web, desktop | Simulates a software-company workplace to evaluate agents on professional tasks involving websites, programs, files, and coworker communication. |
| 2024‑12‑17 | [Proposer-Agent-Evaluator(PAE): Autonomous Skill Discovery For Foundation Model Internet Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.13194) | web | Combines autonomous task proposal, web interaction, and visual success evaluation to discover skills and improve browser-agent policies. |
| 2024‑12‑14 | [WEPO: Web Element Preference Optimization for LLM-based Web Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.10742) | web | Uses web-element preference optimization with sampled negative HTML elements to align high-level user intent with navigation actions. |
| 2024‑12‑13 | [Iris: Breaking GUI Complexity with Adaptive Focus and Self-Refining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.10342) | web, desktop | Combines information-sensitive image cropping with a learning loop between element description and grounding for complex interface understanding. |
| 2024‑12‑12 | [AgentTrek: Agent Trajectory Synthesis via Guiding Replay with Web Tutorials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.09605) | web | Converts public tutorials into task specifications and executes them with an agent to synthesize multimodal interaction trajectories. |
| 2024‑12‑12 | [Falcon-UI: Understanding GUI Before Following User Instructions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.09362) | web, android, cross-platform | Pretrains GUI-context understanding with instruction-free web interactions before fine-tuning Falcon-UI on instructed GUI tasks. |
| 2024‑12‑06 | [The BrowserGym Ecosystem for Web Agent Research](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.05467) | web | Describes BrowserGym's shared web interaction environment and AgentLab tooling for consistent experiments across multiple agent benchmarks. |
| 2024‑12‑02 | [Ponder & Press: Advancing Visual GUI Agent towards General Computer Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.01268) | cross-platform, web, desktop | Separates interpretation of user instructions from precise GUI localization using general-purpose and specialized visual-language components. |
| 2024‑11‑22 | [ScribeAgent: Towards Specialized Web Agents Using Production-Scale Workflow Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.15004) | web | Studies fine-tuning open language models on production web-workflow traces, including training choices for specialized web agents. |
| 2024‑11‑20 | [AdaptAgent: Adapting Multimodal Web Agents with Few-Shot Learning from Human Demonstrations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.13451) | web | Adapts multimodal web agents to unfamiliar websites using a small number of human demonstrations through prompting or meta-learning. |
| 2024‑11‑10 | [Is Your LLM Secretly a World Model of the Internet? Model-Based Planning for Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.06559) | web | Introduces WebDreamer, which simulates candidate web actions with language-model world models and value estimates before acting. |
| 2024‑11‑07 | [Magentic-One: A Generalist Multi-Agent System for Solving Complex Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.04468) | web, desktop | Introduces an orchestrated team of browsing, file, and coding agents with progress tracking and error recovery for general digital tasks. |
| 2024‑11‑06 | [From Novice to Expert: LLM Agent Policy Optimization via Step-wise Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.03817) | web | Introduces StepAgent, which compares agent and expert actions to supply intermediate rewards for policy learning, with implicit-reward and inverse-RL variants. Evaluation includes WebShop and Mind2Web alongside non-web tasks. |
| 2024‑11‑04 | [Attacking Vision-Language Computer Agents via Pop-ups](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.02391) | desktop, web | Tests whether adversarial visual pop-ups divert computer agents from intended tasks in desktop and web benchmark environments. |
| 2024‑11‑04 | [WebRL: Training LLM Web Agents via Self-Evolving Online Curriculum Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.02337) | web | Trains web agents with an evolving task curriculum, outcome supervision, and reinforcement-learning updates intended to manage policy drift. |
| 2024‑10‑31 | [From Context to Action: Analysis of the Impact of State Representation and Context on the Generalization of Multi-Turn Web Navigation Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.23555) | web | Analyzes how interaction history and webpage representation affect multiturn web-agent generalization across unfamiliar websites, task categories, and geographic settings. |
| 2024‑10‑30 | [Evaluating Cultural and Social Awareness of LLM Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.23252) | web | Introduces CASA to assess web agents' responses to cultural and social norms in shopping and forum tasks, including misleading observations. |
| 2024‑10‑29 | [Auto-Intent: Automated Intent Discovery and Self-Exploration for Large Language Model Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.22552) | web | Discovers compact intents from demonstrations and uses intent predictions to guide a language-model web agent's decisions and exploration. |
| 2024‑10‑28 | [AutoGLM: Autonomous Foundation Agents for GUIs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.00820) | web, android | Presents AutoGLM agents for browser and phone control, separating planning from grounding and training through an evolving interaction curriculum. |
| 2024‑10‑25 | [EDGE: Enhanced Grounded GUI Understanding with Enriched Multi-Granularity Synthetic Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.19461) | web, cross-platform | Synthesizes GUI training examples at multiple levels of detail from webpages and studies transfer of learned understanding to other interface environments. |
| 2024‑10‑25 | [OpenWebVoyager: Building Multimodal Web Agents via Iterative Real-World Exploration, Feedback and Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.19609) | web | Develops multimodal browser agents through repeated cycles of real-web exploration, trajectory feedback, and policy optimization. |
| 2024‑10‑24 | [Ferret-UI 2: Mastering Universal User Interface Understanding Across Platforms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.18967) | ios, android, web, cross-platform | Extends Ferret-UI across device platforms with adaptive visual scaling and synthetic data for referring, grounding, and user-centered UI tasks. |
| 2024‑10‑24 | [Infogent: An Agent-Based Framework for Web Information Aggregation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.19054) | web | Infogent separates navigation, extraction and aggregation for web information gathering, including browser interaction through webpage screenshots. |
| 2024‑10‑24 | [VideoWebArena: Evaluating Long Context Multimodal Agents with Video Understanding Web Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.19100) | web | Evaluates whether web agents can retain procedural skills and factual information from long video tutorials and use them during tasks. |
| 2024‑10‑22 | [AdvAgent: Controllable Blackbox Red-teaming on Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.17401) | web | Introduces a black-box web-agent red-teaming framework that learns adversarial prompts from agent feedback to study security weaknesses. |
| 2024‑10‑22 | [Large Language Models Empowered Personalized Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.17236) | web | PersonalWAB benchmarks personalized web tasks, while PUMA retrieves relevant past web behavior to adapt agents' instruction handling and actions. |
| 2024‑10‑21 | [Beyond Browsing: API-Based Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.16464) | web | Compares browsing-only, API-only, and hybrid web agents on WebArena, examining how access to application APIs changes task execution alongside browser interaction. |
| 2024‑10‑17 | [AgentOccam: A Simple Yet Strong Baseline for LLM-Based Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13825) | web | Studies how simplifying and aligning a web agent's observations and actions with language-model capabilities affects task completion. |
| 2024‑10‑17 | [Harnessing Webpage UIs for Text-Rich Visual Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13824) | web | Builds MultiUI by generating instructions from webpage accessibility trees and pairing them with screenshots for multimodal training. | 2024‑10‑17 | [Web Agents with World Models: Learning and Leveraging Environment Dynamics in Web Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13232) | web | Uses a learned web world model to predict important state changes and compare candidate actions before executing them. |
| 2024‑10‑11 | [Refusal-Trained LLMs Are Easily Jailbroken As Browser Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13886) | web | Introduces BrowserART and studies whether chat-model refusal behavior transfers to browser agents carrying out potentially harmful instructions. |
| 2024‑10‑09 | [ST-WebAgentBench: A Benchmark for Evaluating Safety and Trustworthiness in Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.06703) | web | Evaluates web-task completion under explicit enterprise policies, measuring policy-compliant completion and safety violations separately. |
| 2024‑10‑03 | [NNetNav: Unsupervised Learning of Browser Agents Through Environment Interaction in the Wild](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02907) | web | Generates browser-agent demonstrations through autonomous exploration and retrospective task labeling, pruning trajectories that lack meaningful subtasks. |
| 2024‑10‑02 | [ExACT: Teaching AI Agents to Explore with Reflective-MCTS and Exploratory Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02052) | web | ExACT combines reflective tree search with learning from search experience to help web agents explore, assess states and backtrack on VisualWebArena. |
| 2024‑10‑01 | [Multimodal Auto Validation For Self-Refinement in Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.00689) | web | Studies text and visual validation for web agents and uses the resulting validator to detect failures and guide workflow self-refinement. |
| 2024‑09‑24 | [Synatra: Turning Indirect Knowledge into Direct Demonstrations for Digital Agents at Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.15637) | web | Synatra converts indirect procedural knowledge, including human-oriented tutorials, into direct demonstrations for digital agents. | 2024‑09‑23 | [Steward: Natural Language Web Automation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.15441) | web | Introduces Steward, a natural-language browser automation system that repeatedly plans and executes website actions. The paper examines completion detection, state representation, responsiveness, and caching in real-site tasks. |
| 2024‑09‑21 | [StateAct: Enhancing LLM Base Agents via Self-prompting and State-tracking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02810) | web | Maintains explicit task goals and evolving state descriptions to improve base-agent decisions across interactive tasks, including web shopping. |
| 2024‑09‑18 | [The Impact of Element Ordering on LM Agent Performance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.12089) | web, desktop | Studies how the ordering of UI elements in an agent observation affects web and desktop performance. A pixel-based element detector and dimensionality-reduction ordering are evaluated on OmniACT when document hierarchy is unavailable. |
| 2024‑09‑17 | [EIA: Environmental Injection Attack on Generalist Web Agents for Privacy Leakage](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.11295) | web | Evaluates privacy-directed webpage instruction injection against generalist web agents using PII-related interaction steps from Mind2Web, including attempts to expose specific user information or entire requests. |
| 2024‑09‑16 | [NaviQAte: Functionality-Guided Web Application Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.10741) | web | Frames web-application exploration as functionality-oriented question answering, generating navigation actions from multimodal context. |
| 2024‑09‑11 | [Agent Workflow Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.07429) | web | Agent Workflow Memory extracts reusable task routines and selectively supplies them to guide later web actions. | 2024‑09‑06 | [WebQuest: A Benchmark for Multimodal QA on Web Page Sequences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.13711) | web | Introduces question answering over individual screens, multiple web pages, and navigation traces to test information synthesis across views. |
| 2024‑09‑03 | [From Grounding to Planning: Benchmarking Bottlenecks in Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.01927) | web | Separates web-agent planning and grounding evaluations on Mind2Web to analyze which components limit task performance. |
| 2024‑08‑28 | [WebPilot: A Versatile and Autonomous Multi-Agent System for Web Task Execution with Strategic Exploration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.15978) | web | WebPilot separates global subtask planning from local Monte Carlo tree search during web execution. | 2024‑08‑13 | [Agent Q: Advanced Reasoning and Learning for Autonomous AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.07199) | web | Agent Q combines guided tree search, self-critique and preference-based fine-tuning to train web agents from successful and unsuccessful interactions. |
| 2024‑07‑29 | [MindSearch: Mimicking Human Minds Elicits Deep AI Searcher](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.20183) | web | MindSearch uses a planner and searcher agents to split information requests into subquestions and gather answers across web pages. |
| 2024‑07‑23 | [OpenHands: An Open Platform for AI Software Developers as Generalist Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.16741) | web | OpenHands is a platform for agents that write code, use a command line and browse the web, with sandboxed execution and support for agent evaluation. |
| 2024‑07‑22 | [AssistantBench: Can Web Agents Solve Realistic and Time-Consuming Tasks?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.15711) | web | Introduces realistic open-web tasks and the SeePlanAct agent, analyzing the limitations of browsing and retrieval in time-consuming assistant work. |
| 2024‑07‑22 | [IDA: Breaking Barriers in No-code UI Automation Through Large Language Models and Human-Centric Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.15673) | web | IDA helps business users create web UI automation through guided demonstrations, a semantic programming model, and a teaching-oriented interface. A prototype user study examines automation creation with enterprise applications by users without programming backgrounds. |
| 2024‑07‑17 | [Agent-E: From Autonomous Web Navigation to Foundational Design Principles in Agentic Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.13032) | web | Agent-E combines hierarchical web-task control with DOM distillation and observations of interface changes. WebVoyager experiments inform a broader discussion of primitive skills, observation reduction, and self-improvement in agent architecture. |
| 2024‑07‑15 | [Spider2-V: How Far Are Multimodal Agents From Automating Data Science and Engineering Workflows?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.10956) | desktop, web | Benchmarks multimodal agents on data-science and engineering workflows involving enterprise applications, code, and GUI operations in real computer environments. |
| 2024‑07‑07 | [WorkArena++: Towards Compositional Planning and Reasoning-based Common Knowledge Work Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.05291) | web | Defines compositional enterprise workflows to evaluate planning and reasoning, with generation of ground-truth interaction traces. |
| 2024‑07‑01 | [Tree Search for Language Model Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.01476) | web | Applies best-first search to agent actions within interactive web environments, allowing exploration and multistep planning at inference time. WebArena and VisualWebArena experiments examine search behavior and test-time computation. |
| 2024‑06‑20 | [Identifying User Goals from UI Trajectories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14314) | android, web, cross-platform | Defines goal identification from observed UI trajectories and an evaluation method for comparing intent descriptions within the same interface context. | 2024‑06‑19 | [WONDERBREAD: A Benchmark for Evaluating Multimodal Foundation Models on Business Process Management Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.13264) | web | Introduces WONDERBREAD, a benchmark pairing human WebArena workflow demonstrations with screen recordings, action logs, and written procedures. | 2024‑06‑18 | [Dissecting Adversarial Robustness of Multimodal LM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12814) | web | Introduces adversarial tasks on VisualWebArena and an evaluation framework that tracks how manipulated information passes between agent components. The analysis examines vulnerabilities introduced by reflection and tree-search evaluators as well as direct action generation. |
| 2024‑06‑18 | [WebCanvas: Benchmarking Web Agents in Online Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12373) | web | Introduces WebCanvas and Mind2Web-Live, evaluating online task progress through meaningful intermediate states on changing websites. |
| 2024‑06‑14 | [VideoGUI: A Benchmark for GUI Automation from Instructional Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10227) | desktop, web | Evaluates GUI automation from instructional videos at separate levels of high-level planning, action narration, and primitive execution. |
| 2024‑06‑13 | [GuardAgent: Safeguard LLM Agents by a Guard Agent via Knowledge-Enabled Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.09187) | web | Introduces GuardAgent, which translates safety requests into executable guardrail code using plans and retrieved demonstrations. Mind2Web-SC supplies a web-agent safety-policy evaluation alongside a separate healthcare access-control benchmark. |
| 2024‑06‑11 | [CAAP: Context-Aware Action Planning Prompting to Solve Computer Tasks with Front-End UI Only](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.06947) | web | CAAP converts screenshots into textual observations and plans keyboard-and-mouse actions using a prompt that examines task context from multiple perspectives. The agent is evaluated on MiniWoB++ and WebShop using only front-end visual observations. |
| 2024‑06‑01 | [WebSuite: Systematically Evaluating Why Web Agents Fail](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.01623) | web | WebSuite organizes browser operations into an action taxonomy and tests both isolated controls and complete workflows. Its diagnostic tasks attribute failures to specific actions, enabling comparison of text-based and multimodal web agents. |
| 2024‑05‑30 | [Large Language Models Can Self-Improve At Web Agent Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.20309) | web | Examines self-improvement by fine-tuning web agents on three mixtures of model-generated training data. WebArena experiments and trajectory-level evaluation measures study task performance, robustness, and behavior beyond aggregate success rates. |
| 2024‑05‑07 | [Unveiling Disparities in Web Task Handling Between Human and Web Agent](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.04497) | web | Compares human think-aloud web-task behavior with web-agent planning, action, and reflection. The analysis highlights differences in updating plans, handling ambiguity, and investigating failures. |
| 2024‑05‑01 | [Navigating WebAI: Training Agents to Complete Web Tasks with Large Language Models and Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.00516) | web | Combines supervised and reinforcement learning for MiniWoB web tasks and investigates whether agents understand HTML structure or memorize target elements. |
| 2024‑04‑19 | [AutoScraper: A Progressive Understanding Web Agent for Web Scraper Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.12753) | web | Introduces AutoScraper, a two-stage framework that uses HTML structure and similarities across pages to generate scrapers for data collection from websites. |
| 2024‑04‑16 | [Grounded Language Agent for Product Search via Intelligent Web Interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.10887) | web | Introduces GLAINTEL, a Flan-T5-based product-search web agent studied under unsupervised learning, demonstration-based training, and unsupervised domain adaptation. It compares ways to combine demonstrations with reinforcement learning. |
| 2024‑04‑15 | [MMInA: Benchmarking Multihop Multimodal Internet Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.09992) | web | Evaluates multimodal agents on compositional tasks across changing websites and studies replaying prior trajectories as memory support. |
| 2024‑04‑09 | [Autonomous Evaluation and Refinement of Digital Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.06474) | web | Compares automatic trajectory evaluators for web navigation and device control against oracle judgments. The resulting feedback is used for agent fine-tuning and inference-time guidance, including experiments on WebArena. |
| 2024‑04‑09 | [GUIDE: Graphical User Interface Data for Execution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.16048) | web | Introduces annotated website task trajectories with screenshots, prior actions, reasoning, and grounded next actions for process automation. |
| 2024‑04‑09 | [VisualWebBench: How Far Have Multimodal LLMs Evolved in Web Page Understanding and Grounding?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.05955) | web | Separately evaluates web-page reading, understanding, and element grounding across tasks drawn from real websites. |
| 2024‑04‑08 | [WILBUR: Adaptive In-Context Learning for Robust and Accurate Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.05902) | web | WILBUR learns to rank prior task demonstrations for a web agent’s prompt and synthesizes instructions from past runs. A generated task curriculum and a backtracking mechanism support adaptation and recovery, evaluated on WebVoyager. |
| 2024‑04‑04 | [AutoWebGLM: A Large Language Model-based Web Navigating Agent](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.03648) | web | Builds a web agent through simplified HTML observations, human-and-AI browsing data, curriculum training, and reinforcement-learning refinement. |
| 2024‑03‑18 | [Tur\[k\]ingBench: A Challenge Benchmark for Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.11905) | web | Evaluates web agents on multimodal annotation tasks adapted from HTML interfaces originally built for crowdsourcing workers. |
| 2024‑03‑13 | [AutoGuide: Automated Generation and Selection of Context-Aware Guidelines for Large Language Model Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.08978) | web | Introduces AutoGuide, which generates conditional, context-aware guidelines from past agent experience to support decisions in sequential tasks, including web navigation. |
| 2024‑03‑12 | [WorkArena: How Capable Are Web Agents at Solving Common Knowledge Work Tasks?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.07718) | web | Introduces enterprise web tasks on ServiceNow and BrowserGym interfaces for evaluating browser-based agents. |
| 2024‑02‑27 | [OmniACT: A Dataset and Benchmark for Enabling Multimodal Generalist Autonomous Agents for Desktop and Web](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.17553) | desktop, web | Introduces a dataset and benchmark for generating executable task scripts from screenshots and visually grounded desktop or web instructions. |
| 2024‑02‑26 | [WIPI: A New Web Threat for LLM-Driven Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.16965) | web | Studies WIPI, a black-box indirect-instruction threat in which webpage content redirects LLM-driven web agents. Evaluation spans plugin-based agents, Web GPTs, and open-source web-agent implementations. |
| 2024‑02‑23 | [On the Multi-turn Instruction Following for Conversational Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.15057) | web | MT-Mind2Web evaluates web navigation through sequences of conversational user instructions. Self-MAP combines memory-augmented planning with reflection to preserve context and dependencies across those interaction turns. |
| 2024‑02‑12 | [OS-Copilot: Towards Generalist Computer Agents with Self-Improvement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.07456) | desktop, web | Provides an operating-system agent framework connecting applications, files, terminals, and web tools, with accumulated skills for subsequent tasks. |
| 2024‑02‑08 | [WebLINX: Real-World Website Navigation with Multi-Turn Dialogue](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.05930) | web | Collects expert demonstrations of conversational web navigation and studies retrieval-based element selection for processing large pages. |
| 2024‑02‑07 | [UEyes: An Eye-Tracking Dataset across User Interface Types](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.05202) | web, desktop | Introduces UEyes, an eye-tracking dataset of webpage, desktop, mobile, and poster interfaces, and analyzes how people view these different UI types. |
| 2024‑02‑06 | [Dual-View Visual Contextualization for Web Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.04476) | web | Uses screenshot geometry and visual content to enrich HTML element representations for web navigation. Nearby elements contribute textual and visual context, with evaluation on cross-task, cross-website, and cross-domain Mind2Web splits. |
| 2024‑01‑25 | [WebVoyager: Building an End-to-End Web Agent with Large Multimodal Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.13919) | web | Combines multimodal web interaction with a real-website task collection and an automated evaluation protocol. |
| 2024‑01‑24 | [AgentBoard: An Analytical Evaluation Board of Multi-turn LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.13178) | web | Introduces AgentBoard, a multiturn agent evaluation framework with intermediate progress metrics and analysis tools. | 2024‑01‑24 | [VisualWebArena: Evaluating Multimodal Agents on Realistic Visual Web Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.13649) | web | Introduces visually grounded website tasks for evaluating multimodal agents that must interpret both images and text. |
| 2024‑01‑03 | [GPT-4V(ision) is a Generalist Web Agent, if Grounded](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.01614) | web | Introduces SeeAct and live-website evaluation, examining how visual planning and element grounding affect web-agent task completion. |
| 2023‑12‑25 | [WebVLN: Vision-and-Language Navigation on Websites](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.15820) | web | WebVLN trains website navigation from question-based instructions using both rendered visual information and underlying HTML. | 2023‑12‑14 | [CogAgent: A Visual Language Model for GUI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.08914) | desktop, android, web | Introduces a visual-language model with low- and high-resolution image encoders for screenshot understanding and GUI navigation. |
| 2023‑11‑30 | [Exposing Limitations of Language Model Agents in Sequential-Task Compositions on the Web](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.18751) | web | CompWoB adds 50 composed web-automation tasks to study generalization from simpler interactions. The paper compares prompted and fine-tuned agents, balances task training data, and probes sensitivity to changes in instruction order. |
| 2023‑11‑21 | [GAIA: a benchmark for General AI Assistants](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.12983) | web | Introduces questions that test assistant reasoning, multimodal understanding, browsing, and tool use beyond screen-action execution alone. |
| 2023‑10‑24 | [WebWISE: Web Interface Control and Sequential Exploration with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.16042) | web | WebWISE filters DOM elements into observations and generates short programs for click, scroll, and text-entry steps. It evaluates in-context learning from either one supplied example or an example collected during a successful zero-shot MiniWoB++ trial. |
| 2023‑10‑20 | [AllTogether: Investigating the Efficacy of Spliced Prompt for Web Navigation using Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.18331) | web | AllTogether standardizes prompts for HTML-based web navigation and compares prompting with instruction fine-tuning. Its analysis examines how HTML context, interaction history, and current environmental feedback affect task execution. |
| 2023‑10‑16 | [OpenAgents: An Open Platform for Language Agents in the Wild](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.10634) | web | OpenAgents hosts three language agents, for data analysis, API plugins and autonomous web browsing, behind a web interface for non-expert users, with local deployment for developers. |
| 2023‑10‑12 | [A Zero-Shot Language Agent for Computer Control with Structured Reflection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.08740) | web | Studies a zero-shot computer-control agent that plans executable actions and organizes its own reflections after mistakes. MiniWoB++ experiments examine learning to complete tasks without supplied expert trajectories. |
| 2023‑10‑08 | [UReader: Universal OCR-free Visually-situated Language Understanding with Multimodal Large Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.05126) | web | Introduces UReader, which combines unified instruction tuning, auxiliary text-reading tasks and shape-adaptive image crops for OCR-free visual language understanding. Its evaluation includes webpage screenshots alongside documents, tables, charts and natural images. |
| 2023‑10‑05 | [SteP: Stacked LLM Policies for Web Actions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.03720) | web | SteP dynamically composes specialized web-action policies through a stack that records the active chain of policy calls. It studies this control structure across WebArena, MiniWoB++, and CRM workflows. |
| 2023‑09‑15 | [LASER: LLM Agent with State-Space Exploration for Web Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.08172) | web | LASER represents web navigation as exploration over a defined set of states, allowing an agent to revisit earlier states after mistakes. The study evaluates this backtracking approach on WebShop and Amazon tasks. |
| 2023‑07‑25 | [WebArena: A Realistic Web Environment for Building Autonomous Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2307.13854) | web | Introduces reproducible web environments with task-completion checks for evaluating autonomous web agents. |
| 2023‑07‑24 | [A Real-World WebAgent with Planning, Long Context Understanding, and Program Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2307.12856) | web | Combines task decomposition, HTML summarization, and generated Python programs to carry out instructions on websites. |
| 2023‑06‑13 | [Synapse: Trajectory-as-Exemplar Prompting with Memory for Computer Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.07863) | web | Synapse filters task-irrelevant page state and places complete abstracted state-action trajectories into the prompt. An embedding-based exemplar memory retrieves relevant demonstrations for new tasks, evaluated on MiniWoB++ and Mind2Web. |
| 2023‑06‑09 | [Mind2Web: Towards a Generalist Agent for the Web](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.06070) | web | Introduces a web-task dataset spanning diverse websites and evaluates generalization to unseen sites and domains. |
| 2023‑05‑31 | [From Pixels to UI Actions: Learning to Follow Instructions via Graphical User Interfaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.00245) | web | Studies instruction-following agents that observe screenshots and act through a generic mouse-and-keyboard action space, evaluating pixel-based pretraining for GUI tasks on MiniWoB++. |
| 2023‑05‑26 | [AdaPlanner: Adaptive Planning from Feedback with Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.16653) | web | Introduces AdaPlanner, which revises code-structured plans in response to environmental feedback and reuses successful plans as exemplars. Experiments include MiniWoB++ web tasks alongside ALFWorld. |
| 2023‑05‑23 | [Hierarchical Prompting Assists Large Language Model on Web Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.14257) | web | Separates web navigation prompting into a SUMMARIZER that extracts action-relevant observations and an ACTOR that chooses the next action from the condensed context. |
| 2023‑05‑19 | [Multimodal Web Navigation with Instruction-Finetuned Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.11854) | web | Trains WebGUM offline from demonstrations by jointly tuning an instruction-following language model and a vision encoder over webpage screenshots and HTML. The agent predicts browser actions and is evaluated on MiniWoB, WebShop, and Mind2Web. |
| 2023‑03‑30 | [Language Models can Solve Computer Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.17491) | web | Introduces recursive criticism and improvement prompting for language-model agents performing computer tasks, evaluated on MiniWoB++ with a small number of demonstrations per task. |
| 2023‑01‑30 | [WebUI: A Dataset for Enhancing Visual UI Understanding with Web Semantics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2301.13280) | web | Collects rendered webpages and structural metadata for visual UI modeling, studying how web semantics transfer to interface-understanding tasks. |
| 2022‑10‑08 | [Understanding HTML with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.03945) | web | Studies language models for HTML element classification, page description and autonomous web navigation. Fine-tuned models are evaluated on MiniWoB, and a distilled, automatically labeled CommonCrawl HTML corpus supports the training approach. |
| 2022‑10‑07 | [Pix2Struct: Screenshot Parsing as Pretraining for Visual Language Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.03347) | web | Pix2Struct pretrains an image-to-text model by reconstructing simplified HTML from masked webpage screenshots. Its variable-resolution visual representation is evaluated across several domains, including user-interface understanding. |
| 2022‑07‑04 | [WebShop: Towards Scalable Real-World Web Interaction with Grounded Language Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2207.01206) | web | Provides a simulated shopping environment where language agents search, configure, and select products from natural-language requirements. |
| 2022‑02‑16 | [A data-driven approach for learning to control computers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2202.08137) | web | Combines reinforcement learning with behavioral priors from human interactions to learn computer control through keyboard and mouse actions. |
| 2021‑12‑17 | [WebGPT: Browser-assisted question-answering with human feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2112.09332) | web | Trains a question-answering model to search and navigate a text-based web browser, collect references and improve long-form answers using demonstrations and human feedback. |
| 2021‑03‑30 | [Grounding Open-Domain Instructions to Automate Web Support Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2103.16057) | web | Introduces RUSS, which translates support instructions into a domain-specific language and grounds them in actionable web elements. |
| 2021‑01‑23 | [WebSRC: A Dataset for Web-Based Structural Reading Comprehension](https://arxiv.org/abs/2101.09465) | web | Introduces question answering over webpage text and structure, with aligned HTML, screenshots, and metadata for structural reading comprehension. |
| 2019‑02‑19 | [DOM-Q-NET: Grounded RL on Structured Language](https://arxiv.org/abs/1902.07257) | web | Introduces DOM-Q-NET, an RL architecture for web navigation that represents HTML as a tree and uses separate Q-function networks for clicking elements and typing text. |
| 2018‑12‑21 | [Learning to Navigate the Web](https://arxiv.org/abs/1812.09195) | web | Introduces guided RL for QWeb, using a curriculum of smaller instructions and generated tasks to train an agent that follows instructions and fills forms on web pages. |
| 2018‑08‑28 | [Mapping Natural Language Commands to Web Elements](https://arxiv.org/abs/1808.09132) | web | Defines language grounding on webpages as choosing the UI element targeted by a natural-language command. A collection of more than 50,000 commands and three baseline models study functional references, relationships between elements, and visual position cues. |
| 2018‑02‑24 | [Reinforcement Learning on Web Interfaces Using Workflow-Guided Exploration](https://arxiv.org/abs/1802.08802) | web | Uses workflows extracted from demonstrations to constrain reinforcement-learning exploration for interactive web tasks. |
| 2018 | [Rousillon: Scraping Distributed Hierarchical Web Data](https://doi.org/10.1145/3242587.3242661) | web | Introduces Rousillon, which generalizes a demonstration of collecting one hierarchical data row into a browser-automation program that traverses related pages. The paper develops relation-selection and generalization methods and evaluates users building such scripts. |
| 2017‑07‑17 | [World of Bits: An Open-Domain Platform for Web-Based Agents](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v70/shi17a.html) | web | Introduces World of Bits, a web-task platform combining human demonstrations, keyboard and mouse actions, and cached HTTP traffic for repeatable task environments. It evaluates agents trained through behavioral cloning and reinforcement learning. |
| 2016‑11 | [Ringer: Web Automation by Demonstration](https://doi.org/10.1145/2983990.2984020) | web | Introduces Ringer, which converts demonstrated webpage interactions into scripts combining actions, target selectors, and waiting conditions. It evaluates replay robustness on tasks recorded from real websites. |
| 2016‑02‑06 | [End-to-End Goal-Driven Web Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/1602.02261) | web | Introduces WebNav for converting websites into goal-directed hyperlink navigation tasks and WikiNav for evaluating language-conditioned navigation and question answering on Wikipedia. |
| 2007 | [Koala: Capture, Share, Automate, Personalize Business Processes on the Web](https://doi.org/10.1145/1240624.1240767) | web | Introduces Koala, a collaborative web automation system that records browser interactions as editable pseudo-natural-language scripts. Scripts are shared through a wiki and instantiated with personal or corporate data for reuse by other users. |
| 2007 | [PLOW: A Collaborative Task Learning Agent](https://aaai.org/Papers/AAAI/2007/AAAI07-240.pdf) | web | Describes PLOW, which learns executable web-task models from a session combining demonstrations, natural-language explanations, and dialogue. Browser instrumentation connects user actions with DOM structure to support task generalization. |
